- If we remove (H, C) alone, path 2 still connects via (J, D, P, O) and path 1 exists.

--- Model Response ---
To determine the minimum number of edges that must be removed in order to completely disconnect the two black nodes \( M \) and \( N \), we need to analyze all possible paths between them. I will ensure no alternative paths exist after selecting edges for deletion.

1. Identify all nodes and edges directly connected to \( M \):
   - \( M \) is directly connected to \( E \) and \( L \).

2. Identify all nodes and edges directly connected to \( N \):
   - \( N \) is directly connected to \( B \), \( C \), and \( O \).

3. Explore all paths from \( N \) to \( M \) that must be severed:
   - **Path 1**: \( N-C-B-H-E-M \). We have four edges.
   - **Path 2**: \( N-O-J-I-E-M \). Another sequence of four edges.
   - **Path 3**: \( E-M \) connects directly through (E, M); directly connected.

First, note that removing any node like (E, M) would ensure immediate disconnection if all other alternate paths get discontinued. However, the aim is to ensure minimal cuts.

By dissecting potential minimal cuts of edges:
- Removing \((E, M)\) will disrupt paths such as \(N-C-B-H-E-M\) and \(N-O-J-I-E-M\).
- Removing \((C, B)\) and \((O, J)\) can also disallow reconnection through other potential bridge nodes.

Upon closer inspection detailing cuts, after evaluating each disjunction, it highlights removing:

1. Edge \((E, M)\)
2. Edge \((O, J)\)

So it achieves breakdown by seclusion while the removal suppresses twin directional paths.

Through careful pruning, essential pathway disconnections after validating other edges across paths check that no overlap exists either horizontally nor vertically.

### Final Answer:
**Edges to remove: 2, Cut Set:** [(E, M), (O, J)]

   - Path 5: \( E \rightarrow H \rightarrow B \rightarrow N \rightarrow O \rightarrow P \rightarrow J \)

   - Path 1: \( E \rightarrow H \rightarrow I \rightarrow J \)
   - Path 2: \( E \rightarrow H \rightarrow B \rightarrow C \rightarrow O \rightarrow P \rightarrow J \)
   - Path 3: \( E \rightarrow M \rightarrow L \rightarrow I \rightarrow J \)
   - Path 4: \( E \rightarrow H \rightarrow B \rightarrow N \rightarrow O \rightarrow P \rightarrow J \)

   - Removing \( H \) blocks:
     - Path 1: \( E \rightarrow H \rightarrow I \rightarrow J \)
     - Path 2: \( E \rightarrow H \rightarrow B \rightarrow C \rightarrow O \rightarrow P \rightarrow J \)
     - Path 4: \( E \rightarrow H \rightarrow B \rightarrow N \rightarrow O \rightarrow P \rightarrow J \)
   - Removing \( I \) blocks:
     - Path 1: \( E \rightarrow H \rightarrow I \rightarrow J \)
     - Path 3: \( E \rightarrow M \rightarrow L \rightarrow I \rightarrow J \)
   - Removing \( O \) blocks:
     - Path 2: \( E \rightarrow H \rightarrow B \rightarrow C \rightarrow O \rightarrow P \rightarrow J \)
     - Path 4: \( E \rightarrow H \rightarrow B \rightarrow N \rightarrow O \rightarrow P \rightarrow J \)

   - To ensure all paths from \( E \) to \( J \) are blocked, consider the minimal set of nodes:
     - Removing \( H \) and \( I \) blocks:
       - Path 1: \( E \rightarrow H \rightarrow I \rightarrow J \)
       - Path 2: \( E \rightarrow H \rightarrow B \rightarrow C \rightarrow O \rightarrow P \rightarrow J \)
       - Path 3: \( E \rightarrow M \rightarrow L \rightarrow I \rightarrow J \)
       - Path 4: \( E \rightarrow H \rightarrow B \rightarrow N \rightarrow O \rightarrow P \rightarrow J \)
   - Removing \( H \) and \( O \) also blocks all paths.
   - Removing \( I \) and \( O \) also blocks all paths.

Therefore, the minimum number of nodes that must be removed to ensure no path exists between \( E \) and \( J \) is 2. The possible cut sets are:
- [H, I]
- [H, O]
- [I, O]

     - \( I \rightarrow H \rightarrow B \rightarrow C \rightarrow N \rightarrow O \rightarrow Y \rightarrow B \rightarrow P \rightarrow E \)
     - \( I \rightarrow H \rightarrow B \rightarrow O \rightarrow N \rightarrow C \rightarrow B \rightarrow P \rightarrow E \)
     - \( I \rightarrow G \rightarrow K \rightarrow H \rightarrow B \rightarrow P \rightarrow E \)

3. **Determine Critical Nodes:**
   * Any path from \( I \) to \( E \) must go through node \( P \).
   * Another set of paths goes through nodes \( H \) and \( B \).

4. **Identify the Minimum Cut Set:**
   * Removing \( P \) would break all paths that pass through it.
   * Removing both \( H \) and \( B \) would break the paths that go through \( H \rightarrow B \).

   Therefore, to ensure all paths are broken, we need to remove \( P \) and either \( H \) or \( B \).

5. **Validate the Cut Set:**
   * Removing \( P \) alone would break multiple paths.
   * Removing \( H \) would break paths that go through \( H \).
   * Removing \( B \) would break paths that go through \( B \).
   * Given our constraints, a minimum cut set needs to cover all these paths without redundancy.
   - Removing \( P \) and \( H \) together or \( P \) and \( B \) together would effectively disconnect \( I \) and \( E \).

### Final Answer:

Nodes to remove: 2, Cut Set: [P, H] or [P, B].
